I just can't seem to make myself do the next step while it's happening.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes, do you suddenly feel intense fear or panic that reaches its worst within a few minutes?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel scared or panicked like that. I just... get stuck. It's not out of fear, it's just like my brain won't let me move on to the next thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about many different things most days?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm a big worrier. It's really just this weird getting stuck thing that's the problem.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes, do you feel unable to control what you’re doing?\n\nPatient: Not exactly out of control, no. Um... it's not like my body is moving on its own or anything like that. I know exactly what I'm doing. It's more just... I can't seem to push past it to do the next step. So I wouldn't say I've lost control of my actions, I'm just stuck on that one thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you have intrusive thoughts or images that keep coming into your mind and feel hard to get rid of?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's not thoughts popping up that I can't get rid of.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes, do you ever feel detached from yourself, like you’re watching yourself from outside your body?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't ever felt like that. It's not out of fear, it's just like my brain won't let me move on to the next thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about many different things most days?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm a big worrier. I mean, I have normal stress, like paying bills or whatever, but I don't spend most days worrying about a bunch of different things. It's really just this weird getting stuck thing that's the problem.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes, do you feel unable to control what you’re doing?\n\nPatient: Not exactly out of control, no. Um... it's not like my body is moving on its own or anything like that. I know exactly what I'm doing. It's more just... I can't seem to push past it to do the next step. So I wouldn't say I've lost control of my actions, I'm just stuck on that one thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you have intrusive thoughts or images that keep coming into your mind and feel hard to get rid of?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't get random thoughts or pictures stuck in my head. Um, my mind is pretty quiet in that way. It's really just getting stuck on the actual thing I'm doing, like wiping down a table at work or whatever. It's not thoughts popping up that I can't get rid of.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes, do you ever feel detached from yourself, like you’re watching yourself from outside your body?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't ever felt like that. I'm definitely in my body, doing the thing. I just... I just can't stop doing it. But no, I've never felt like I was watching myself from the outside or anything.\n\nDoctor: Before you get stuck doing the same action, do you feel a strong urge that you have to do it to relieve anxiety or prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: No, it's not like that either. I don't feel anxious beforehand, and I'm not worried about something bad happening if I don't do it. It's just... I start doing a task, like wiping the counter, and then I just keep doing it. There's no big urge or fear driving it. I just get stuck.\n\nDoctor: When you get stuck repeating the action, do you feel like you have to do it to prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: No, like I was just saying, it's really not about preventing anything bad. I don't have any thoughts like that in my head when it happens. I'm just wiping the table, and then I keep wiping it. There's no reason behind it, I just can't seem to stop the movement once I start.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever repeat these actions because you’re worried about germs or contamination?\n\nPatient: No, it's not about germs at all. I mean, I wipe tables at work all day, so I'm used to cleaning up, but... I don't feel worried about getting sick or anything being dirty. It's really just the movement itself that I get stuck on, not why I'm doing it.\n\nDoctor: Before or during these episodes, do you feel a sudden urge or warning sensation right before you repeat the movement?\n\nPatient: No, I don't get any kind of warning sensation. It's not like a sudden urge hits me or anything. I just... I don't feel it coming on at all. I'll just be in the middle of doing a normal movement, and then I realize I'm stuck doing it. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess... my boss actually told me I needed to come see someone. Um, what scared everyone at work is that I started freezing up in the middle of service. Or I'd start doing things completely out of order. Like, the other day, I was just standing by the soda station holding the cash drawer key, and I was just repeating table numbers over and over instead of actually closing out the checks. I don't really know why I did it, but it freaked them out.\n\nDoctor: When those episodes happen, do you later have trouble remembering what you were doing or what happened?\n\nPatient: No, I actually remember it fine. Um, it's not like I black out or anything. I know I'm standing there holding the key, and I can hear myself saying the numbers. I just... I don't really know how to explain it, but my memory of it is perfectly clear. I just can't seem to make myself do the next step while it's happening.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes, do you suddenly feel intense fear or panic that reaches its worst within a few minutes?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel scared or panicked like that. I just... get stuck. It's not out of fear, it's just like my brain won't let me move on to the next thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about many different things most days?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm a big worrier. I mean, I have normal stress, like paying bills or whatever, but I don't spend most days worrying about a bunch of different things. It's really just this weird getting stuck thing that's the problem.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes, do you feel unable to control what you’re doing?\n\nPatient: Not exactly out of control, no. Um... it's not like my body is moving on its own or anything like that. I know exactly what I'm doing. It's more just... I can't seem to push past it to do the next step. I don't get random thoughts or pictures stuck in my head. Um, my mind is pretty quiet in that way. I don't have any thoughts like that in my head when it happens. I'm just wiping the table, and then I keep wiping it. There's no reason behind it, I just can't seem to stop the movement once I start.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever repeat these actions because you’re worried about germs or contamination?\n\nPatient: No, it's not about germs at all. I mean, I wipe tables at work all day, so I'm used to cleaning up, but... I don't feel worried about getting sick or anything being dirty. It's really just the movement itself that I get stuck on, not why I'm doing it.\n\nDoctor: Before or during these episodes, do you feel a sudden urge or warning sensation right before you repeat the movement?\n\nPatient: No, I don't get any kind of warning sensation. It's not like a sudden urge hits me or anything. I just... I don't feel it coming on at all. I'll just be in the middle of doing a normal movement, and then I realize I'm stuck doing it. There's really nothing that happens right before.\n\nDoctor: Before you start repeating the movement, do you ever feel a sudden build-up of tension or urge that gets relieved when you do it?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. Like I said, there's no feeling or urge beforehand. I don't feel any tension building up, and doing it doesn't relieve anything. It's really just... I'm doing something, and then I can't stop doing it. There's no build-up to it.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes, do you ever feel like you’re in a trance or like your sense of who you are changes?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I don't feel like I'm in a trance or anything. I'm still just... me. I know exactly who I am and what's going on around me when it happens. Like, if I'm at the restaurant, I know I'm just standing there wiping the same spot on a table, but I just can't stop doing it. My sense of who I am doesn't change at all.\n\nDoctor: Before the repeating movement starts, do you ever feel a brief urge or build-up in your body that you have to let out?\n\nPatient: No, I really don't. I know you've asked about an urge or tension a couple of times now, but... no, there's nothing like that. I don't feel a build-up in my body that I have to let out. It just happens without any kind of warning or feeling beforehand.\n\nDoctor: Do you have repetitive movements that seem purposeless or automatic, even when you’re not trying to do them?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Actually, that's what scared everyone at work recently. I started just... freezing up right in the middle of service, or doing things completely out of order. Like, instead of closing out checks, I was just standing by the soda station holding the cash drawer key. And I was just repeating table numbers out loud, over and over. I wasn't trying to do it, it just... happened automatically.\n\nDoctor: Right before an episode starts, do you ever get any strange sensation like a smell, taste, déjà vu, or a rising feeling in your stomach?\n\nPatient: No, I don't get anything like that. I just can't seem to make myself do the next step while it's happening.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes, do you suddenly feel intense fear or panic that reaches its worst within a few minutes?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel scared or panicked like that. I just... get stuck. It's not out of fear, it's just like my brain won't let me move on to the next thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about many different things most days?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm a big worrier. I mean, I have normal stress, like paying bills or whatever, but I don't spend most days worrying about a bunch of different things. It's really just this weird getting stuck thing that's the problem.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes, do you feel unable to control what you’re doing?\n\nPatient: Not exactly out of control, no. Um... it's not like my body is moving on its own or anything like that. I know exactly what I'm doing. It's more just... I can't seem to push past it to do the next step. I don't get random thoughts or pictures stuck in my head. Um, my mind is pretty quiet in that way. It's really just getting stuck on the actual thing I'm doing, like wiping down a table at work or whatever. It's not thoughts popping up that I can't get rid of.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes, do you ever feel detached from yourself, like you’re watching yourself from outside your body?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't ever felt like that. I'm definitely in my body, doing the thing. I just... I just can't stop doing it. But no, I've never felt like I was watching myself from the outside or anything.\n\nDoctor: Before you get stuck doing the same action, do you feel a strong urge that you have to do it to relieve anxiety or prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: No, it's not like that either. I don't feel anxious beforehand, and I'm not worried about something bad happening if I don't do it. It's just... I start doing a task, like wiping the counter, and then I just keep doing it. There's no big urge or fear driving it. I just get stuck.\n\nDoctor: When you get stuck repeating the action, do you feel like you have to do it to prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: No, like I was just saying, it's really not about preventing anything bad. I don't have any thoughts like that in my head when it happens. I'm just wiping the table, and then I keep wiping it. There's no reason behind it, I just can't seem to stop the movement once I start.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever repeat these actions because you’re worried about germs or contamination?\n\nPatient: No, it's not about germs at all. I mean, I wipe tables at work all day, so I'm used to cleaning up, but... I don't feel worried about getting sick or anything being dirty. It's really just the movement itself that I get stuck on, not why I'm doing it.\n\nDoctor: Before or during these episodes, do you feel a sudden urge or warning sensation right before you repeat the movement?\n\nPatient: No, I don't get any kind of warning sensation. It's not like a sudden urge hits me or anything. I just... I don't feel it coming on at all. I'll just be in the middle of doing a normal movement, and then I realize I'm stuck doing it. There's really nothing that happens right before.\n\nDoctor: Before you start repeating the movement, do you ever feel a sudden build-up of tension or urge that gets relieved when you do it?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. I'm doing something, and then I can't stop doing it. There's no build-up to it.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes, do you ever feel like you’re in a trance or like your sense of who you are changes?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. My sense of who I am doesn't change at all.\n\nDoctor: Before the repeating movement starts, do you ever feel a brief urge or build-up in your body that you have to let out?\n\nPatient: No, I really don't. It just happens without any kind of warning or feeling beforehand.\n\nDoctor: Do you have repetitive movements that seem purposeless or automatic, even when you’re not trying to do them?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. So, no, my memory is fine afterwards.\n\nDoctor: Have these episodes started after a major stress or traumatic event?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. There hasn't been any big trauma or anything like that lately. I mean, work can be stressful sometimes—I'm a waitress, so it gets busy—but nothing major or out of the ordinary happened before this started. It just kind of began on its own."
